### 2026-06-28 — `@balance commands`: command utilization + the "Huh?"-friction rate finally has a consumer — *drop telemetry-command-rollup*
T3.19 telemetry READ-SIDE quality — the **same producer→consumer integrity class the last five fires each peeled a layer of** (`ff2032d` chain, `375d1e9` session, `1c46d9a` skill_check, `cbfb765` economy/cp_spend, `923e3dd` progression), each resolved best/most-complete by **adding the consumer, not dropping the producer**. The progression fire's own follow-up note named **two** genuinely-distinct producers still read-side-dropped (`command` + `craft`); this drop closes the **`command` slice**. `parser/commands.py::_emit_command_telemetry` has long emitted one `command` event per invocation at the **single dispatch chokepoint** — `matched` (bool; False = an unknown command, the friction the NPE drive fights), `cmd` (the canonical command key for a resolved command so aliases/glued prefixes group together, or the typed word for an unknown one), and optional `typed`/`sw`/`char_id`. The emit-site docstring states its whole purpose: the command-frequency histogram (catalog C — a dead verb shows ~0 use), alias-vs-canonical usage, and the unknown-command "Huh?" friction rate (catalog D — which verbs confuse). But the CONSUMER half was missing: `engine/telemetry.summarize()` rolled up grind/cp/objective/wild_encounter/communal/chain/session/skill_check/economy/progression and **silently dropped `command` on the floor**, and `@balance` had no command board — so the exact NPE-friction signal the whole fun-drive fought (and which commands players actually use) appeared nowhere but the generic event-mix count + the `raw` dump. ### 2026-06-28 — `@balance flows` + CP-spend: the credit-economy read-side finally has its consumers — *drop telemetry-economy-rollup*
T3.19 telemetry READ-SIDE quality, the same producer→consumer integrity class the last three fires each peeled ONE layer of (`ff2032d` chain, `375d1e9` session, `1c46d9a` skill_check — each resolved best/most-complete by **adding the consumer, not dropping the producer**). This fire closes the **credit-economy slice of the gap in one coherent drop** after an audit found `summarize()` rolled up only **9 of the 26 emitted event types** — eleven dedicated producers that EMIT but whose data `summarize()` silently dropped on the floor (appearing nowhere but the generic event-mix count + the `raw` dump): the entire credit faucet/sink cluster — `vendor_txn` / `commissary_txn` / `gamble` / `debt` / `gear_insurance` / `housing` / `den` / `medical_treatment` / `pc_bounty` / `entertainer_perform` — and the CP **sink** `cp_spend` (the `train` advancement spend, paired with the already-rolled-up `cp_income` faucet). Two consumers added: (1) a new **`economy`** rollup → **`@balance flows`** board — per-system **gross credit throughput** (events + credits moved), biggest-mover first, via a small total `_economy_credits()` extractor that knows each producer's heterogeneous credit-field schema (`price` for vendor, `cost`/`refund` for commissary, `bet` for sabacc, `paid` for medical, `payout` for entertainer, signed `amount` for debt/insurance/housing/den/bounty). Deliberately **gross, not net**: these emitters record only the ACTING player's side of a possibly-two-sided transfer, so a summed net would mislead — gross volume per system is the honest "which systems move money and how much" tuning signal for each system's prices/fees/payouts knobs. (2) the existing **`cp`** rollup gains the `cp_spend` sink (`spends`/`cp_spent`/`by_skill`), so the renamed **CP ECONOMY** board now shows income **and** spend — the hoard-vs-spend / where-CP-goes signal. Additive read-side ONLY — no emit-path / gameplay / faucet / sink / schema change; `engine/director.py` untouched (avoid-lane); touched `engine/telemetry.py` + `parser/director_commands.py` (the `@balance`/T3.19 lane); era-clean. The new `flows` sub-board lands its two **self-maintaining** doc guards in lockstep (both derive the canonical sub-board list from the live `usage` string): `@balance.md` help + Guide_27 §3 both gain the `flows` entry (named **`flows`** not `economy` precisely so it stays distinct from the `@economy` command the help/guide contrast — and so the substring-based help guard can actually verify it's documented). New `tests/test_telemetry_economy_rollup.py` (28): the rollup buckets the REAL producer field names per domain (gross summed, biggest-mover-ordered); `_economy_credits` is pinned against each live credit-field name (a producer renaming its field fails loudly here, not silently); the `_ECONOMY_DOMAINS` map is pinned to the credit-producer set; junk/missing amounts → 0 (never crash); the `economy` + cp-spend keys are additive (all eight sibling rollups unperturbed); the boards render/alias/gate/degrade; and — the load-bearing contract — the REAL `_emit_debt`/`_emit_housing`/`_emit_gear_insurance`/`_emit_den` + `_emit_cp_spend` producers feed the consumers end-to-end through `read_recent` → `summarize` → the board. NOTE for a follow-up fire: six NON-credit producers are still read-side-dropped (`faction_rep`, `influence`, `vanity_title`, `spacer_quest`, `craft`, `command`) — a separate progression/social-breadth board, not credit-economy. Targeted gate green (263 across new + chain/session/skill_check siblings + guide-27 + help-balance).

### 2026-06-28 — Design-call queue hygiene: close out the verified-resolved Force-sensitive chargen BLOCKER — *drop force-fork-closeout*
Launch readiness is partly about Brian's `design_calls_pending_brian` queue being *signal* — and it was carrying a false positive. `FORCE.chargen_sensitivity_representation` sat there as a `status: OPEN`, **"BLOCKER"**-titled fork, yet its own `implementation_note` recorded it as **IMPLEMENTED 2026-06-22** (drop `sidebar-contract-handoff-capture`) with only the `pending->resolved` status flip "left to conductor per spec" — which got orphaned when that interactive session ended days ago. Per the no-phantom hard invariant (verify, never trust a note), this drop verified the **entire cluster at HEAD** before closing it, then completed the bookkeeping. **Verified at HEAD:** (a) BLOCKER — `server/api.py` `handle_submit` + `handle_create_character` write `DicePool(1, 0)` not `DicePool(0, 0)` for a Force-sensitive char's `control`/`sense`/`alter` (`L594-596` / `L1092-1094`); (b) HIGH — `parser/padawan_master_training_commands._ensure_padawan_skills_one_die` writes the raised Force skill to the **attributes** blob, not the skills column (`L513`); (c) MEDIUM — `parser/cp_commands.TrainCommand` intercepts `control`/`sense`/`alter` with a `+teach`/master teacher-redirect instead of a silent "Unknown skill" (`L198-204`); (d) MEDIUM — `data/worlds/clone_wars/test_character.yaml` Force attrs moved to top-level attributes. **The shipped fix is WEG-canonical and strictly less invasive than the staged recommendation:** WEG R&E gives a Force-sensitive character **1D** in control/sense/alter at creation ("Ana receives control at 1D and learns one power"), so the producers were writing the *wrong value* (`0D` is correctly the non-sensitive sentinel), not the *representation* being wrong. The fix therefore **preserves** CLAUDE.md's `force_sensitive is DERIVED from control/sense/alter key presence` hard invariant — **no schema column, no derivation change** — because a non-zero 1D pool derives `force_sensitive=True` correctly via `engine/character.from_db_dict` while the opposing L2 0D-lockout fix (`0D` = non-sensitive) stays valid. **Test-pinned + GREEN at HEAD:** `tests/test_qa_force_sensitive_chargen_2026_06_22.py` (both endpoints write 1D + roundtrip `force_sensitive=True` + `list_powers_for_char` non-empty; +teach attrs-write + reconstruction; train-redirect for all 3 Force skills; normal-skill non-interception; non-FS chargen regression pin) **plus** the opposing `tests/test_qa_l2_force_0d_lockout.py` + `tests/test_l2_force_0d_lockout_fix.py` — **32 tests pass together** (run this drop). Moved the fork `pending_brian -> resolved_recent` with a full resolution + an explicit **off-ramp** (re-open if you still want the explicit-persisted-flag form). **Bookkeeping only — no code change** (the implementation already shipped 2026-06-22); pending queue 11 -> 10.

Unknown command`, while the *tutorial's* smart "I didn't catch that / Try: …" recovery (added in the NL-redirect drop) only fired on question-like input. Two fixes in `parser/commands.py`, neither of which adds command aliases (so the command-syntax-rework's canonical-verb decisions are untouched — this only upgrades routing + the error): (1) **`go`/`walk`/`head <dir>`** now route to `MoveCommand` (the universal text-game movement reflex; MoveCommand resolves compass + named exits and lists the room's exits on a miss), joining the existing bare-direction + named-exit routing; (2) **every in-game unknown command** now flows through the helpful recovery (was gated to `?`/question-word/3+-word input) — "I didn't catch that." + the active objective + command-to-type (when on a tutorial step) + "Type help … (Ctrl/Cmd+K searches commands)." Pre-login (no character) users still get the crisp `Huh? Unknown command`. Updated `tests/test_named_exit_routing.py` (typo now gets recovery + a new `go <exit>` routing test) and `tests/test_nl_confusion_redirect.py` (single-word + reflexive-word unknowns now route to recovery). NOTE: still a DESIGN FORK for Brian — whether to add real `inventory`/`inv`/`i` etc. ALIASES (the rework deliberately deleted them for `+inv`); this drop makes the dead-end *helpful*, it does not re-add the aliases.

### 2026-06-27 — Graduation guidance handoff: the GOALS panel now catches the new graduate — *drop fun11-goals-handoff*
8th fun re-run #1 kills-it (the new layer once fun6/7/8/10 made the whole tutorial completable + FUN — scores jumped to 4/4/4/3/4/4, would-keep-playing TRUE, open world reached for the first time): at the exact handoff to open play ("CHAIN COMPLETE — the galaxy is open"), the GOALS sidebar stayed BLANK, so the freshly-graduated player holding the accepted "First Deployment" mission had no surface telling them what to do or where to go — a guidance vacuum right where the guided tutorial spine ends. Root cause: `Session._hud_sidebar_goals` (server/session.py) applied `is_chain_mission_visible_to` — a BOARD-offering filter — to the player's ALREADY-ACCEPTED mission, which returns false once the NPE chain completes, so the mission slot emptied and (with no questline/bounty yet) the panel early-returned to nothing. Fix: an ACCEPTED mission is the player's active objective and always surfaces in GOALS regardless of chain-step visibility (the visibility filter stays for which chain missions to OFFER on the board, not for displaying an accepted one). Live-verified: post-graduation the GOALS panel now renders `display:block` with "MISSION | 300 cr | First Deployment: Coruscant Industrial District | … OPEN · +missions". Server-only; no faucet/sink/schema. New `tests/test_fun11_goals_handoff.py` (2: accepted chain-mission surfaces post-graduation; empty board still suppresses the panel).

### 2026-06-27 — Guide_16 re-verify: the combat sim auto-resolves — spam `attack` to win, no posing ritual — *drop guide16-fun10-autopose-reverify*
GUIDES re-verify lane (Opus-owned) — reconciled the onboarding-critical Guide_16 (now the `help`-landing guide at foundations order 1) to the fun10-sim-autopose change (`56d63b0`) it had silently drifted behind (the curated suite never reads guide prose). fun10 fixed the 7th-re-run step-2 soft-lock: a sim round only resolves once every fighter poses, so a newcomer's instinct — type `attack` again — stacked the WEG multi-action penalty (-1D each) in an unresolved ROUND 1 until the pool hit 0D and every swing auto-missed forever. The fix auto-poses pending PLAYERS in an `is_simulation` room (via `_auto_pose_sim_players` in `parser/combat_commands.py::_start_posing_window`), so each declared `attack` resolves immediately and spamming `attack` wins. But Guide_16 §4 Step 2 still said "the engine drives you through declaration → resolution → **posing**" — presenting posing as a step the learner goes through, i.e. the exact ritual they got stuck on, the opposite of the new behavior. Reconciled §4 Step 2: it now teaches that the sim short-circuits posing for you (contrasted with real combat, where a round waits for every fighter to pose), so "you don't need to learn posing yet — just keep typing `attack` and you'll wear the droids down and win," with a note that bare `attack` also auto-targets the nearest droid so the single word carries the whole fight. Version 1.5→1.6. Docs+test only; `parser/combat_commands.py` READ-ONLY (stayed clear of the concurrent fun-drive combat/parser/client avoid-lane); era-clean, no schema/faucet/sink. Extended `TestCombatSimSafeSandbox` in `tests/test_guide_16_tutorial_chains.py` (4 new): the stale "declaration → resolution → posing" framing must stay dead; §4 Step 2 must teach "keep typing `attack`" + "don't need to learn posing yet"; and a producer guard pinning the live `_auto_pose_sim_players` + its `is_simulation`-gated call in `_start_posing_window`, so a regression re-arming the multi-action freeze fails loudly against the guide's promise. Targeted gate green (guide-16 + hygiene + smoke-foundation).

### 2026-06-27 — Sim combat auto-resolves: spamming `attack` WINS the drill instead of soft-locking — *drop fun10-sim-autopose*
7th fun re-run #1 kills-it (a DIFFERENT step-2 trap than fun6's stun fix): the tutorial combat round only resolves on a `pass`/pose, but a newcomer's instinct — type `attack` again — declares ANOTHER action in the SAME unresolved round, stacking the WEG multi-action penalty (-1D each) until the dice pool hits 0D and every swing auto-misses, frozen in ROUND 1 forever (live-reproduced: 24× `attack` → ROUND 1, penalty -22D, 0 kills, the absurd "your attack and your attack ×22" pose prompt). `pass` (HUD-labelled "Hold action") just skips the turn so droids never die; the only winning ritual (`attack` THEN `pass` ×10) is undiscoverable. Fix: in an `is_simulation` room the posing window now auto-poses pending PLAYERS too (as it already does NPCs) via new `_auto_pose_sim_players` in `parser/combat_commands.py::_start_posing_window`, so a declared attack resolves immediately — each `attack` is its own resolved round and spamming `attack` WINS. Multi-action stacking can't accumulate (each declaration resolves before the next). Scoped to is_simulation: real combat's declare/pose/multi-action flow is untouched. Live-verified: 16× bare `attack` → ROUND advances to 6, penalty 0, drill complete → **STEP 3/5** (was a hard freeze). New `tests/test_fun10_sim_autopose.py` (3). NOTE: the general declare-then-resolve UX + the mislabelled PASS button ("Hold action" when a declare is pending) remain a real polish item for non-sim combat — folds into combat-UX, not this tutorial-unblock.

### 2026-06-27 — Graduate reward loop: every CW chain graduate now escapes into the world AND can reach a vendor — *drop fun7-reward-loop*
Resolves the spend-path half of the `FUN.shop_verb` fork — but the verified gap was far bigger than "name a buy verb": an audit of all 8 CW profession-chain graduations found **every** chain dropped the graduate into a vendorless pocket, and **7 of 8 were 0-exit `tutorial_zone` rooms** — i.e. a graduation-stranding soft-lock (the tutorial's *end* was as broken as its middle, just never reached until fun6 unblocked the drill). The legacy core-tutorial seed pocket got an "escape into city" link in 2026-06-24; the CW profession chains never did. Fix (mirrors that pattern; the buyer verb `buy <item>` already worked via `BuyCommand` open-market, which sells the global `vendor_stocked` commons in any room holding an `ai_config.vendor` NPC — a real, pre-existing producer): (1) **retarget** the 7 stranding chains' `graduation.drop_room` (chains.yaml) to live navigable hubs — republic_soldier/intelligence + separatist_agent → `coco_town`, separatist_commando → `mos_eisley_market_district`, bounty_hunter + smuggler → `nar_shaddaa_promenade_main`, shipwright_trader → `kuat_ring_commercial` (jedi_path / jedi_path_independent already land in live rooms, unchanged); (2) **author 3 era-clean WEG-D6 starter outfitters** (`npcs_drop_fun7_starter_vendors.yaml`, additive era.yaml include) — Brekka Solwynn (Zabrak, coco_town), Muss Farren (Devaronian, Nar Shaddaa promenade), Orvak Tesh (Sullustan, Kuat commercial) — each `ai_config.vendor:true` + 4D+ Bargain, placed where the planet had no reachable faction-appropriate vendor (Tatooine reuses Kayson/Lup/Jawa; Kuat's existing Lira Shan is a questline-tied ship broker, so Orvak is the dependable gear seller). Now a graduate lands with ~400-800 credits in a live hub and can immediately `buy` blaster/comlink/medpac. No new faucet (graduation credits already shipped); the vendors are the missing SINK. Map-safe: chains.yaml carries zero coordinates / no golden-snapshot guard; the new NPC file + era.yaml line are additive (zero deletions). New `tests/test_fun7_graduate_economy_reachable.py` (4: world has vendors; every profession drop_room resolves; every profession graduate reaches a vendor; none stranded in a 0-exit room) — sibling to the legacy seed-pocket guard. The verb-rename half of the fork (still) defers to the planned command-syntax-rework.

### 2026-06-27 — Telemetry read-side: the `@balance` dashboard now surfaces the tutorial-chain / questline completion funnel — *drop telemetry-chain-rollup*
OpusLoop quality lane (T3.19 telemetry breadth — the read-side gap, not another questline). The onboarding/questline reward pipeline already EMITS a `chain_reward` telemetry event at every reward-bearing step (phase=`step`) and ALWAYS at graduation (phase=`graduation`) — the emit-site comments in `engine/chain_rewards.py` call it the load-bearing NPE metric ("chain completion rate per chain_id"), and it is fully tested on the producer side (`test_t319_chain_reward_telemetry.py`). But the **consumer half was missing**: `engine/telemetry.summarize()` rolled up grind / cp_income / objective / wild_encounter / communal and silently DROPPED `chain_reward` on the floor, and the `@balance` admin dashboard had grind/cp/objectives/encounters/events sub-boards but **no chain board** — so the funnel those events were emitted FOR appeared nowhere except the generic event-mix count and the `raw` dump. With twelve freelance questlines + five trainer trials now live (the T3.24 launch content this lane has been hardening), the completion/credit-flow signal Brian wants for post-launch tuning was being captured to disk and never read. This drop adds the missing consumer (best/most-complete: add the consumer, don't drop the producer — same pattern as the auth_status / rank_up producer fixes): `summarize()` gains a per-chain `chain` rollup — `graduations` (the true per-chain completion count, since graduation always emits), reward-bearing `step_events`, total credit/item/achievement flow, and a `by_chain` breakdown keyed by chain_id carrying the graduation's human label; and `BalanceCommand` gains a `@balance chains` sub-board (rendered in the overview too, completion-ordered, label preferred over the raw id) plus the help/usage line. New `tests/test_telemetry_chain_rollup.py` (15): pins the rollup against the REAL producer field names (per-chain graduations/steps/credits/label, junk + unknown-phase tolerance, the chain key is additive so the sibling rollups are unperturbed), the board's rendering + gating (absent under other subs, degrades cleanly with no chain data), and — the load-bearing contract — drives the REAL `apply_graduation_rewards` producer through flush → `read_recent` → `summarize` → the board end-to-end. Additive read-side only — no emit-path / gameplay / faucet / sink / schema change; `engine/director.py` untouched (avoid-lane); era-clean.

### 2026-06-27 — Balance-integrity guard: freelance-questline SKILL CHECKS are now pinned WINNABLE for a fresh character — *drop questline-skill-difficulty-winnability*
OpusLoop quality lane. The third leg of the questline winnability/integrity trilogy — its siblings pin the economy axis (`test_questline_reward_tier_consistency`) and the COMBAT axis (`test_questline_foil_winnability_band`, the entry below); this one pins the SKILL-CHECK axis. Every T3.24 freelance accessible questline (`kind: questline`, chain_id not `master_`-prefixed, gated only on `chargen_complete`) advances through one or more `skill_check_passed` steps, each carrying an authored `skill:` and an integer `difficulty:` rolled at `chain attempt` via `perform_skill_check(char, skill, difficulty)`. Two properties of that difficulty are load-bearing for a fresh player and **nothing pinned them**: (1) MAGNITUDE — the arcs open to a brand-new character were authored inside a tight Easy→Moderate band (shipped range 9..14, all ≤ WEG Moderate=15), and a fat-fingered Difficult/Heroic value (`difficulty: 25`) on a `chargen_complete`-only arc is a practical **soft-lock** (retries are cooldown-throttled; a starting 2D–3D pool can't roll 25 in any reasonable number of attempts) — the same open-world soft-lock class the combat-sim drive spent days fixing, but on the skill axis; (2) RETRYABILITY — every freelance skill check ships `on_fail: retry_allowed`, and an arc authored with a terminal no-retry on_fail would strand a player who fails one roll. The unit suite is blind to both because the per-arc slice harness force-passes skill checks (never rolls dice). New `tests/test_questline_skill_difficulty_winnability.py` (18): difficulty bands are sourced from the game's OWN resolver ladder (`engine.dice.Difficulty` — VERY_EASY=5/EASY=10/MODERATE=15/DIFFICULT=20, so the guard tracks the engine if those re-tune, no phantom constant; WEG40120 is gitignored/absent in-worktree). Asserts each freelance skill check has a present int difficulty (else `chain attempt` shows the "misconfigured" staff message = soft-lock), sits ≤ MODERATE(15) (the cliff guard, hard absolute), ≥ Easy(6) (anti-vacuous floor), and `on_fail == retry_allowed`; a self-calibrating intra-arc ramp check (difficulties non-decreasing across an arc's steps — a later step easier than an earlier one is the signature of an order/value fat-finger); a corpus-wide backstop (BOTH tiers ≤ DIFFICULT(20) — even a t5 trainer trial is never Heroic); and contract-sanity + negative checks (the ceiling clears the shipped max with headroom; a planted 25 / Very-Easy 3 / `abort_step_no_retry` / non-int difficulty each trips the matching predicate, so the cover isn't false). Skill-NAME validity is already covered by the reachability invariant's CLASS 4 (this guard does not re-cover it). Deliberate asymmetry vs the reward guard (same rationale as the foil guard): magnitude ceilings are hard absolutes because a difficulty ceiling is a winnability cliff — a deliberate galaxy-wide rebalance SHOULD trip it and force a conscious edit. Pure test guard — no engine, parser, data, or client change; era-clean; no faucet/sink/schema.

### 2026-06-27 — Balance-integrity guard: freelance-questline COMBAT foils are now pinned WINNABLE for a fresh character — *drop questline-foil-winnability-band*
OpusLoop quality lane. The companion to the reward-tier guard, on the other axis content can silently break: combat difficulty. Eleven of the twelve T3.24 freelance accessible questlines end on a `combat_won` step against a single hand-authored antagonist NPC tagged `ai_config.chain_enemy_template` (Sealed Ledger #9 is deliberately combat-free). Those foils were all stat-matched to one tight "antagonist band" — blaster ~4D, dodge ~3D+2, brawling ~4D+1, Strength ~3D+1, a pistol sidearm — so a fresh `chargen_complete` character with a blaster can always win the confrontation beat. But **nothing pinned that band.** Each per-arc slice test only walks its own combat step through the harness (which auto-resolves the fight without rolling dice); none asserts the foil is actually WINNABLE for a real player. Since this lane *grows a freelance arc most fires* (each authored from a sibling), a single fat-fingered foil — a `blaster: 6D`, a `dodge: 5D`, a `blaster_rifle` in place of the pistol — would ship a combat step a new player physically **cannot beat: a soft-lock in the open world**, the exact failure class the tutorial combat-sim drive spent days fixing (the sim was unwinnable — see the fun6-sim-winnable entry below). The unit suite is blind to it because the harness never rolls dice; this guard catches it at build time on data alone. New `tests/test_questline_foil_winnability_band.py` (10): loads every foil from `npcs_drop_generalized_questline_*.yaml`, and asserts each sits under an absolute "fair fight" ceiling — to-hit (blaster) ≤ 5D, defense (dodge) ≤ 4D+1, melee (brawling) ≤ 5D, toughness (Strength) ≤ 4D, sidearm in {blaster_pistol, heavy_blaster_pistol} — with a light to-hit FLOOR (≥ 3D+1) so the beat stays a real fight, plus a self-calibrating cluster-consistency check (per-stat spread across the corpus ≤ 4 pips, so a coordinated band shift passes but a lone outlier surfaces) and producer-sanity (every foil is `hostile`; the ceilings provably clear the shipped maxima so the pass isn't vacuous). Deliberate asymmetry vs the reward guard: the reward guard self-calibrates because rewards drifting up together is harmless, but a combat ceiling is a winnability CLIFF, so these ceilings are hard absolutes — a galaxy-wide difficulty rebalance SHOULD trip the guard and force a conscious one-line edit. Pure test guard — no engine, parser, data, or client change; era-clean; no faucet/sink/schema.

### 2026-06-27 — Economy-integrity guard: questline reward TIERS are now pinned for cross-arc consistency — *drop questline-reward-tier-consistency*
OpusLoop quality lane. The T3.24 questline corpus has two reward tiers — the 12 **freelance** accessible side-jobs (every non-`master_` `kind: questline`: chargen_complete-gated, Independent-aligned, canonical TOTAL arc reward 450 credits + 17 Independent rep + a cp_reward-3 achievement) and the 5 **t5 master-trainer trials** (`master_*`: 700 credits + ~17 faction rep). Every arc was already individually within band — the per-slice tests assert total credits ≤ 1000 and the corpus-wide `test_t5_questline_content.TestChainRepEconomyCeiling` enforces faction_rep below HONORED (50) / under the tuned CEILING (22) across all chains — but **nothing pinned reward consistency *across* the arcs of a tier.** A new accessible questline could ship 800 cr / 20 rep — inside every existing band — yet be wildly out of line with the 12 peers that all pay an identical 450/17, and the whole suite would stay green. Since this lane *grows a freelance arc most fires* (each authored from a sibling), a single fat-fingered reward is the realistic drift; this guard makes it fail loudly. New `tests/test_questline_reward_tier_consistency.py` (10): freelance tier pays identical total credits + identical faction_rep (Independent-only, under ceiling) + a shared achievement cp_reward; t5 tier pays identical total credits; the two tiers stay distinct (freelance < t5); and the tier partition is non-vacuous. The assertions are **self-calibrating** — they read the canonical value from the tier itself and require the rest to agree, so a deliberate balance pass that re-tunes a whole tier together still passes (conservative-on-balance); only a single arc drifting from its peers fails. The literal 450/700 figures are documentation, not the assertion. Pure test guard — no engine, parser, data, or client change; era-clean; no faucet/sink/schema.

### 2026-06-27 — Onboarding: a new web player who types `help` now lands on the new-player guide, not the dense dice reference — *drop web-help-onboarding-landing*
Path-to-first-fun quality fix (Opus-owned), found while running down the fun-drive's open "bare `help` shows nothing" lead. That lead is NOT a server/render bug — it's an AI misattribution (same class as the documented feed-resync one): in the web client a bare `help` (and `guide`/`+guide`) is intercepted client-side and opens the in-game **Guide overlay** (`openGuideBrowser` in `static/client.html`), a modal the fun-pass DOM probe — which reads the feed/room panels — wouldn't see as content. Verified end-to-end the overlay genuinely works: `_load_guides()` populates 24 player-visible guides (access_level 0), `GET /api/portal/guides` returns them for an anonymous/PLAYER caller, and the overlay renders the list + auto-loads the first guide. So `help` shows plenty — but it showed the WRONG thing first. The overlay auto-loads `data.guides[0]` (the first index entry, sorted by category-order then intra-order), and that landing guide was **WEG D6 Core Mechanics** — the densest dice-pool / wild-die / difficulty-number reference in the whole corpus. The single most natural "I'm lost" command for a brand-new player dropped them onto a wall of dice math instead of "here's what to do." Fix is pure guide frontmatter: the **foundations** category is reordered into a new-player journey — **Tutorial Chains & First Character (1)** → Character Creation (2) → WEG D6 Core Mechanics (3) → CP Progression (4) — so `guides[0]` (the `help` landing) is now Guide_16, *the* onboarding guide (its own summary: "the mandatory onboarding path for new players"), which opens with a welcoming, self-orienting "How to Read This Guide" rather than dice tables. Data-only: two `order:` swaps (Guide_16 3→1, Guide_01 1→3), Guide_16 summary gains "Start here."; no engine/parser/client behavior changed (stayed clear of the concurrent combat/parser fun-drive session), era-clean, no schema/faucet/sink. The existing reorganization test already enforces orders stay positive + unique-within-category + ascending-sorted, so the swap is safe; new `tests/test_web_help_onboarding_landing.py` (8) pins the contract end-to-end — server side: the onboarding guide is foundations order 1 AND is `_GUIDE_INDEX[0]` (the auto-load target), the dense reference is NOT index[0], foundations orders stay a contiguous 1..N; client side (source guard): `openGuideBrowser` auto-loads `data.guides[0]` and bare `help` still routes to the overlay — so a future frontmatter shuffle that re-buries the onboarding guide (or re-promotes a dense reference) fails loudly. Targeted gate green (188: new slice + guides-reorganization + in-game-browser + guide-01 + guide-16; + hygiene + smoke-foundation).

### 2026-06-27 — Guides re-verify: Guide_16 reconciled to the now-WINNABLE combat sim (sim hit is STUN-only, never a KO) — *drop guide16-sim-winnable-reverify*
GUIDES re-verify lane (Opus-owned), reconciling the new-player guide to the fun6-sim-winnable drop (`576631d`, shipped earlier today). That fix changed the Tipoca Combat Sim's safety guarantee a second time: where the fun3 drop let a sim hit **KO** the player (a brief auto-clearing knockout) and capped wounds at STUNNED/KO, fun6 found the KO + the **uncapped** stun (−1D per active stun) were exactly what HARD soft-locked step 2/5 — a solo learner KO'd or buried under ~23 stuns could never finish the drill. So a sim PC hit now NEVER sets `unconscious_until` (no KO out of the fight) and keeps at most `_SIM_STUN_CAP=1` active stun (a mild, non-compounding −1D). But Guide_16 — *the first thing a brand-new player reads* — still told that nervous newcomer a droid hit "can stun or **briefly knock you out**" (§4 Step 2) and "can stun or **knock you out for a moment**" (§8). That is now precisely backwards: being knocked out is the soft-lock the fix removed, and the prose re-scares the learner with the one outcome that can no longer happen. Reconciled both passages: a sim hit can **briefly stun** you (a mild, capped setback the simulation never lets pile up), but you are **never knocked out of the fight**, wounded, scarred, or killed — so you can always keep acting until you win. Version 1.4→1.5. Docs+test only; `engine/combat.py` READ-ONLY (stayed clear of the concurrent combat/parser fun-drive session); era-clean, no schema/faucet/sink. Extended `tests/test_guide_16_tutorial_chains.py` `TestCombatSimSafeSandbox`: new `test_no_stale_knockout_claim` (the two old affirmative "knock you out" phrasings must stay dead), the non-lethal test now also requires the positive "never knocked out of the fight" guarantee, a new `test_sim_stun_is_capped_and_never_kos` pins `_SIM_STUN_CAP` is a small positive int AND is wired into `CombatInstance._apply_damage` (so a regression re-arming the KO / uncapped stun fails loudly against the prose), and the stale "STUNNED/KO" block comment is corrected. 74 guide-16 tests green.

### 2026-06-27 — Fix the tutorial soft-lock: the sim drill is now WINNABLE (no KO, capped stun) — *drop fun6-sim-winnable*
5th-fun-re-run #1 kills-it (deterministic across 3 runs): once the disambiguation fix let players cleanly engage both B1 sim droids, the Republic Soldier tutorial HARD soft-locked at STEP 2/5 — the drill never completed, no "drill complete" cue, exits gone (tutorial rooms teleport on advance), every new player trapped. Root cause found by live probe (NOT a `combat_won` registration bug — that works): my fun3 sim safety had two flaws. (1) The margin>3 sub-path KO'd the player for **2D minutes** (`unconscious_until`) — a solo learner KO'd mid-drill can't finish. (2) Worse, a sim hit STUNS every round and the WEG stun penalty is **−1D per active stun** (`len(stun_timers)`), uncapped — so it compounds without bound; a probe showed the player kill Alpha, then take **23 stuns** and be unable to land a single hit on Bravo (~−23D) → drill unwinnable → step stuck forever. Fix (Brian's "at most stunned" taken to its intent — a *winnable* safe sandbox): in an `is_simulation` room a PC hit now (a) NEVER sets `unconscious_until` (no KO), and (b) keeps at most `_SIM_STUN_CAP=1` active stun (a mild, non-compounding −1D the learner fights through). Live-verified end-to-end: player now kills BOTH droids (ALPHA✓ BRAVO✓), "Sims passed", **STEP advances 2/5 → 3/5** and teleports back to Major Tarrn — the first-fun path is unblocked through the drill. `engine/combat.py` only (the sim wound-cap branch); NPCs still take full damage; non-sim combat unchanged. `tests/test_fun3_sim_safety.py` +2 (never-KO'd; stun-accumulation capped) → 21 green. NOTE: also reconfirmed the `combat_won` cumulative-kill accumulation is sound (Alpha registered fine); the soft-lock was purely the offense-crippling stun, not the chain hook.

### 2026-06-27 — De-LLM the tutorial-critical talk: bound the NPC-dialogue call so a hung Ollama can't stall the chain — *drop fun5-talk-dellm*
4th-fun-re-run reliability finding: `talk Major Tarrn` (the tutorial's first required action) advanced the chain + teleported in most runs but was intermittently SILENT with no advance — the chain hook (`on_talk_to_npc` in `_post_talk_hooks`) runs AFTER the LLM reply in `TalkCommand._generate_and_display`, and `await brain.dialogue(...)` had NO timeout, so a hung/very-slow Ollama call awaited until the 30s dispatcher `COMMAND_TIMEOUT` cancelled the whole command — skipping the chain advance entirely (a silent step-1 stall). Fix: hard-bound the single dialogue call with `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=NPC_DIALOGUE_TIMEOUT_S=12s)` + an `asyncio.TimeoutError` branch that uses the existing `_get_fallback()` line. Now a stalled LLM falls back in ≤12s and the command (and the post-talk chain advance + teleport after it) always completes — the directed-say broadcast already gave the instant ack, and exceptions were already caught; this closes the HANG gap. Engine-only (`parser/npc_commands.py`); no schema/faucet/sink; era-clean. New `tests/test_fun5_talk_timeout.py` (3: a 5s-hung dialogue is bounded <2s + falls back; a normal reply is used; the constant is sanely under the command budget); 87 existing talk/npc/dialogue tests green. NOTE (verified, NOT bugs): the 4th re-run's "empty combat-HUD" is a post-combat-inspection artifact (`handleCombatState` DOES show the strip on active + `renderCombatFeed` reveals `#combat-feed` on swing events, then clears on combat-end — the tutorial fight ends in 1-2 rounds so it's empty when inspected; dice render inline meanwhile), and GOALS is intentionally NPE-suppressed (drop ux-drop6-goals) — neither was churned.
### 2026-06-27 — T3.24 generalized questline: "The Driven Herd" (twelfth accessible side-questline — first on KAMINO, first Strength-skill / beast-rider arc) — *drop generalized-questline-driven-herd*
T3.24 generalized quest expansion (Opus-owned right-of-launch lane), twelfth slice — author MORE questlines on the proven pattern per `quest_expansion_postlaunch_path_v1.md`. "The Driven Herd" (`kamino_driven_herd`) is the TWELFTH accessible (non-t5, non-tutorial) `kind: questline` chain, after The Ghost Shipment, The Crooked Wheel, The Lost Courier, The Skimmed Line, The Dust-Sick, The False Provenance, The Forged Notice, The Warrens Toll, The Sealed Ledger, The Sabotaged Run, and The Hollow Crew. It reuses the SAME live questline engine (`engine/tutorial_chains.py` + `chain_events.py`: the `kind: questline` type + `active_questline` slot, the existing event types, the four reward funnels) with **NO new engine code, no new slot, no new event type, no schema migration, no new reward faucet, and NO map-coordinate or exit-graph change** — pure additive content.
- **Two firsts for the accessible-questline arc:** (1) a **TWELFTH distinct skill spread — BEAST RIDING + SWIMMING** — neither of which any prior accessible questline uses (vs search+streetwise / investigation+gambling / persuasion+sneak / security+bargain / first_aid+survival / value+con / forgery+bureaucracy / command+demolitions / pick-pocket+hide+computer-programming / sensors+repulsorlift-operation / alien-species+droid-programming). `beast riding` resolves to **Mechanical** and `swimming` to **Strength** via the canonical `perform_skill_check` funnel; neither is aliased (each canonicalizes to itself). This is the **FIRST accessible questline whose spread includes a STRENGTH skill at all** — every prior arc's spread drew only on Perception / Knowledge / Technical / Mechanical — and the **FIRST to reward BEAST RIDING**: the physical creature-handler build (the rider-and-swimmer) that the social, investigative, appraisal, leadership, finesse, slicer, pilot, and xeno-scholar arcs never serve. A dedicated test pins that a char who trained Swimming rolls their TRAINED pool at the live `chain attempt`, not raw Strength, that the spread shares no skill with any of the prior eleven, and (grounded against `skills.yaml`) that no prior-arc spread skill maps to Strength. (2) the **FIRST questline set on KAMINO** — the offworlder-accessible ocean-platform margin (`kamino_aiwha_stable` / `kamino_ocean_surface` / `kamino_smuggler_dock`).
- **The Tipoca City cloning facility and the clone army are kept ENTIRELY OFF-STAGE.** The whole arc runs on the faction-neutral fauna-and-spacer margins of the open-ocean platforms (the Aiwha Stable, the Ocean Surface Platform, the sensor-shadowed Unauthorized Docking Point beneath the city) — the Clone War kept offstage exactly the way every prior accessible arc keeps the Republic/CIS conflict offstage. Era-clean ~20 BBY, no canon figures (no Lama Su / Taun We / Kit Fisto).
- **New story shape** — breaking a wildlife-poaching racket / rescuing a driven herd, not a smuggling-trace, racket-bust, kidnap-rescue, industrial-skim, frontier rescue, art-fraud caper, counter-forgery, community-defense, finesse heist, fixed race, or droid-reflash racket. No prior accessible arc touches fauna at all. A poacher, Korsa Drael, works the ocean platforms with a stolen Kaminoan herd-call goad: in the storm-squalls he sounds it to panic the working aiwha off their roost and scatter them into the open swell, where his crew nets the foundered ones and crates them — under the city's sensor umbrella, off the logs — for off-world collectors; the aiwha his crew don't reach drown, and the keeper's hired string thins one squall at a time. You gentle one of the driven beasts foundering near the ocean-surface platform and ride it out to where the herd was taken (`beast riding`), dive the poacher's submerged net-pen and cut the foundering aiwha free before they drown (`swimming`), stand off the poacher when he comes back to a pen cut empty (`attack`/`dodge`), and give the stranded platforms their herd back.
- 5 steps (talk → `beast riding` check diff 11 → `swimming` check diff 13 → `combat_won` **climax** → talk) reusing existing event types; **TWO** original era-clean NPCs — **Neyla Sur**, a Nautolan aiwha-keeper, the non-hostile GIVER + closer at the Aiwha Stable (step-1 + step-5); and **Korsa Drael**, a Weequay poacher, the step-4 `combat_won` foil at the Unauthorized Docking Point (`ai_config.chain_enemy_template driven_herd_poacher`, hostile). The off-world buyers, the poacher's net-crew, the stolen herd-call goad, the scattered and netted aiwha, and the stranded platform outfits are **narrated-only props** — story beats, not spawned NPC entities; the aiwha are the planet's established native megafauna (narrated, never statted as combat entities). All step `location` slugs are real loaded Kamino ocean-platform rooms (`planets/kamino.yaml`); the NPC file is wired into `era.yaml content_refs`.
- Accessible — any post-chargen character can take it (prerequisites: `chargen_complete`; no faction rep gate), runs in normal Kamino ocean-platform rooms, faction-neutral (independent), and pays a modest reward (300 cr graduation + a one-time step-4 150 cr = 450 cr; 17 `independent` rep < the tuned 22 ceiling) + a lower-tier achievement (`driven_herd_freed`, `cp_reward` 3, matching the prior eleven vs the t5 trainer chains' 5). Era-clean ~20 BBY, WEG R&E D6 stats — Nautolan (the amphibious aquatic species of Glee Anselm, the natural fit for an ocean-world creature-keeper and strong swimmer) and Weequay (a faction-neutral Outer Rim hunter/mercenary species), both era-clean and not war-faction coded; the giver matched to the non-combat fixer band (with the keeper's spread skewed Mechanical/Strength to fit a beast-handler and amphibious swimmer) and the poacher to the small-time-criminal antagonist band (Brakkis Vone / Gorvahn Brusk / Skarn Vex / Suro Vant / Vohrek Sarn / Brask Yade / Varn Tesk / Tarko Venn / Drevin Saar / Vossk Rhal).
- **Guide reconcile (no guide drift ships):** Guide_16 §15's freelance-questline table is bumped "Eleven"→"Twelve" with a new row (The Driven Herd / Aiwha Stable, Kamino ocean platform / Neyla Sur) plus a clause that one freelance arc now hands the work to a Strength build (the rider and the swimmer), and the guide-16 guard test's `_FREELANCE_QUESTLINES` map gains the entry — so the new content lands with the new-player guide already matching HEAD.
- New `tests/test_generalized_questline_driven_herd.py` (24) walks start→graduation through the production dispatcher; pins accessibility, the reward band (graduation credits exactly 300), the registered+linked achievement, real Kamino room slugs, the `beast riding`+`swimming` skill spread, the twelfth-distinct-spread guard (no overlap with the prior eleven), the Mechanical+Strength grounding against `skills.yaml` (incl. the first-Strength-skill claim), the trained-pool resolution, the combat-climax structure (single foil + enemy_template + wrong-template no-advance), the all-Kamino-rooms (first arc on the planet) invariant, and the two-placed-NPC shape. The generic `test_t5_questline_content.TestAllQuestlinesWalkable` + the static reachability invariant auto-cover this questline too. Targeted gate green (slice + sibling regressions + walkability + reachability + Guide_16 guard + hygiene + smoke-foundation).

### 2026-06-26 — Questline discoverability: `mastery browse` galaxy-wide directory — *drop questline-directory*
Quality/right-of-launch lane (Opus). With 15 questlines now live (5 master-trainer + 10 accessible freelance arcs), discoverability — not volume — was the binding constraint: the only way to learn a questline existed was to physically stand with its giver NPC and `talk` (`get_questline_offer` is room-scoped). Scattered across Nar Shaddaa / Tatooine / Coruscant / Kuat / Geonosis, nearly all of them were invisible — real launch content nobody could find. This drop surfaces the whole catalog. **Pure-additive, read-only; extends the existing `mastery` command (the questline system's own home), NOT the separate random-job mission board.**
- **New engine read `engine/chain_events.list_questline_directory(char, era=None)`** — a galaxy-wide directory of every `kind: questline` arc, annotated with the character's standing and partitioned ACTIVE → AVAILABLE → LOCKED → COMPLETED (corpus order within each group). Each entry carries `{chain_id, chain_name, archetype_label, faction_alignment, giver_npc, start_zone, start_location, objective, status, reason}`. Pure read (no DB/I-O); reuses the SAME `list_questlines` + `is_chain_locked_for_character` + `has_completed_chain` gate the in-room offer uses, so the directory and a per-NPC `talk` offer never disagree about eligibility.
- **New consumer `mastery browse`** (`parser/questline_commands.py`; aliases `all` / `directory` / `catalog`) renders the directory with the giver, starting zone, and the exact `mastery start <id>` per available arc; locked arcs are dimmed with their gate reason; completed arcs listed compactly. **Surfaces NO capability `mastery start` didn't already grant** — `start_questline` already validates the gate and teleports from anywhere, so the only thing previously hidden was the EXISTENCE of an id, not access to it; `start` still enforces every gate. An active questline pins to the top with the one-at-a-time caveat ("finish your active questline first" replaces the bare start lines on the others).
- **Bare-`mastery` no-offer fallback** now points a stranded player at `mastery browse` instead of dead-ending with "keep exploring."
- **Guide reconcile (no drift):** Guide_16 §15 explicitly claimed *"You don't go to a quest board — there isn't one"* — now false. Reworked §15 "How you find one" to teach both paths (the `mastery browse` directory + the in-context `talk` offer), added `mastery browse` to §15's command block and the §16 quick-reference table.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_questline_directory.py` (12) — the engine helper enumerates the whole corpus and partitions by standing; the partition agrees with the gate AND the in-room `get_questline_offer` (consistency); the one-at-a-time rule surfaces; `mastery browse` renders the directory + every available start id; the `all`/`directory`/`catalog` aliases route to it; the bare-`mastery` fallback points at browse. Guide_16 guard green (72). Era-clean, no faucet/sink (read-only discoverability), no schema change.

### 2026-06-26 — T3.24 generalized questline: "The Sabotaged Run" (tenth accessible side-questline — first MECHANICAL-build / pilot-and-scanner arc) — *drop generalized-questline-sabotaged-run*
T3.24 generalized quest expansion (Opus-owned right-of-launch lane), tenth slice — author MORE questlines on the proven pattern per `quest_expansion_postlaunch_path_v1.md`. "The Sabotaged Run" (`tatooine_speeder_sabotaged_run`) is the TENTH accessible (non-t5, non-tutorial) `kind: questline` chain, after The Ghost Shipment, The Crooked Wheel, The Lost Courier, The Skimmed Line, The Dust-Sick, The False Provenance, The Forged Notice, The Warrens Toll, and The Sealed Ledger. It reuses the SAME live questline engine (`engine/tutorial_chains.py` + `chain_events.py`: the `kind: questline` type + `active_questline` slot, the existing event types, the four reward funnels) with **NO new engine code, no new slot, no new event type, no schema migration, no new reward faucet, and NO map-coordinate or exit-graph change** — pure additive content.
- **Two firsts for the accessible-questline arc:** (1) a **TENTH distinct skill spread — SENSORS + REPULSORLIFT OPERATION** — none of which any prior accessible questline uses (vs search+streetwise / investigation+gambling / persuasion+sneak / security+bargain / first_aid+survival / value+con / forgery+bureaucracy / command+demolitions / pick-pocket+hide+computer-programming). (2) the **FIRST accessible questline to lead with a MECHANICAL build**: both spread skills resolve to the Mechanical attribute, so it is the first side-arc to reward a pilot / driver / scanner character — every prior accessible arc leads with Perception, Knowledge, Technical, or combat skills and touches Mechanical not at all. Both skills route through the canonical `perform_skill_check` funnel; neither is aliased (each canonicalizes to itself), and a dedicated test pins that a char who trained Repulsorlift Operation rolls their TRAINED pool at the live `chain attempt`, not raw Mechanical.
- **Third, vehicle-country face of Tatooine** — the Mos Eisley outskirts speeder circuit (`spaceport_speeders` / `transport_depot` / `outskirts_speeder_track` / `outskirts_wrecked_sandcrawler`), reusing **NONE** of the two prior Tatooine arcs' rooms (the Crooked Wheel's market-district `market_place_geps_grill` / `chalmuans_cantina_main_bar` / `mos_eisley_market_district` / `outskirts_abandoned_farm` or the Dust-Sick's Jundland `outskirts_scavenger_market` / `jundland_hidden_cave` / `jundland_dune_sea_edge` / `jundland_beggars_canyon` — pinned by a dedicated no-overlap test).
- **New story shape** — breaking a fixed racing circuit. A swoop gang the outskirts calls the Dust Wheelers has the speeder circuit fixed: they scan honest riders' repulsors at the start gates and spike the coils of anyone who won't buy "track insurance," so their own rigs take every purse and haul contract and the independents fold. This week they spiked a young runner mid-race and stripped her rig at their chop-shop in a gutted sandcrawler, a contracted medical-supply run for the far moisture farms stranded on it. You read the gang's spike off the depot gate-scanner logs and trace their rig (`sensors`), run the circuit to pull their outrider off the chop-shop road (`repulsorlift operation`), stand off the enforcer who keeps the chop-shop (`attack`/`dodge`), and bring the rig and the run home.
- 5 steps (talk → `sensors` check diff 11 → `repulsorlift operation` check diff 13 → `combat_won` **climax** → talk) reusing existing event types; **TWO** original era-clean NPCs — **Ria Tann**, a Duros speeder-shop mechanic, the non-hostile GIVER + closer at Spaceport Speeders; and **Drevin Saar**, a Devaronian gang enforcer, the step-4 `combat_won` foil at the Wrecked Sandcrawler chop-shop (`ai_config.chain_enemy_template sabotaged_run_enforcer`, hostile). The gang's boss **"the Pacer"**, the beaten outrider, the chop-shop crew, the young runner whose rig was taken, and the stripped rigs are **narrated-only props** — story beats, not spawned NPC entities. All step `location` slugs are real loaded Tatooine rooms, none reused from the two prior Tatooine arcs; the NPC file is wired into `era.yaml content_refs`.
- Accessible — any post-chargen character can take it (prerequisites: `chargen_complete`; no faction rep gate), runs in normal Tatooine outskirts rooms, faction-neutral (independent), and pays a modest reward (300 cr graduation + a one-time step-4 150 cr = 450 cr; 17 `independent` rep < the tuned 22 ceiling) + a lower-tier achievement (`sabotaged_run_won`, `cp_reward` 3, matching the prior nine vs the t5 trainer chains' 5). Era-clean ~20 BBY, WEG R&E D6 stats — Duros (the galaxy's ubiquitous spacer/pilot/mechanic species) and Devaronian (a faction-neutral rogue/scoundrel fixture of Outer Rim cantinas), both era-clean and not war-faction coded; the giver matched to the non-combat fixer band (with the mechanic's spread skewed Mechanical/Technical) and the enforcer to the small-time-criminal antagonist band (Brakkis Vone / Gorvahn Brusk / Skarn Vex / Suro Vant / Vohrek Sarn / Brask Yade / Varn Tesk / Tarko Venn).
- **Guide reconcile (no guide drift ships):** Guide_16 §15's freelance-questline table is bumped "Nine"→"Ten" with a new row (The Sabotaged Run / Spaceport Speeders, Mos Eisley / Ria Tann) plus a clause that one freelance arc now hands the wheel to a pilot or scanner, and the guide-16 guard test's `_FREELANCE_QUESTLINES` map gains the entry — so the new content lands with the new-player guide already matching HEAD.
- New `tests/test_generalized_questline_sabotaged_run.py` (24) walks start→graduation through the production dispatcher; pins accessibility, the reward band (graduation credits exactly 300), the registered+linked achievement, real room slugs, the `sensors`+`repulsorlift operation` skill spread, the **combat-climax** structure (single foil + enemy_template + wrong-template no-advance), the **Mechanical-build-first** claim (both spread skills grounded against `skills.yaml`'s `mechanical:` list), the trained-pool resolution, the no-overlap-with-prior-Tatooine-arcs invariant, and the two-placed-NPC shape. The generic `test_t5_questline_content.TestAllQuestlinesWalkable` + the static reachability invariant auto-cover this questline too. Targeted gate green (slice + sibling regressions + walkability + reachability + Guide_16 guard + achievements + f8-chains + hygiene + smoke-foundation).

- **Two firsts for the accessible-questline arc:** (1) a **NINTH distinct skill spread — PICK POCKET + HIDE + COMPUTER PROGRAMMING** (slicing) — none of which any prior accessible questline uses (vs search+streetwise / investigation+gambling / persuasion+sneak / security+bargain / first_aid+survival / value+con / forgery+bureaucracy / command+demolitions). `pick pocket` resolves to Dexterity, `hide` to Perception, and `computer programming` to Technical via the canonical `perform_skill_check` funnel — `computer programming` is the one aliased name (`computer programming` → `computer programming/repair` through `engine.character._SKILL_KEY_ALIASES`), and a dedicated test pins that a char who trained Computer Programming/Repair rolls their TRAINED pool at the live `chain attempt`, not raw Technical. (2) a **NEW STRUCTURE — the FIRST accessible questline that resolves on FINESSE, not force**: three skill-check steps and **NO combat step**, climaxing on the master slice rather than a blaster fight. (The static reachability Class-5 anti-vacuous combat check stays satisfied corpus-wide by the tutorial chains + the eight prior combat-climax questlines.)

An info-broker the mid-city knows only as the Ledger runs a forgery mill dressed as a "debt-clearing service": he fabricates lien-records against working families, seizes their accounts through a registry desk he controls, and keeps the originals (the proof the liens are fake) in a private data-vault hidden in a decommissioned corner of the Coruscant Works, on a purge schedule that re-forges the records to beat any audit. An honest records-advocate can't out-appeal a man who owns the desk, so you lift the vault fob off his collector in the transit crush (`pick pocket`), slip past his watchers and the dead sublevel's sensor sweep (`hide`), and crack the slicer-gauntlet on the data-core before the purge wipes it (`computer programming`) — then carry the original ledger to an honest desk so the forged liens collapse all at once.

### 2026-06-26 — Guides re-verify: Guide_26 §3 documents the dark-side cult-uprising player loop — *drop guide26-cult-uprising-reverify*
GUIDES re-verify lane (Opus-owned). The **events-as-playable-scenarios** rework (`events-playable-scenarios` + `events-more-scenarios`, 2026-06-24) turned the dark-side cult events from a global menace *counter* into a real, fightable system — a visible menace meter, a `rally` threat board, and for **three** cults a staged location scenario you travel to and `investigate` (fight waves → work the objective → bring down the leader). That shipped *after* Guide_26's last pass, and the guide corpus documented the live `rally` loop **nowhere** — worse, Guide_26 §3 framed *all* dark-side beats as pure Director narration ("don't surface as a tracked number"), which the menace meter + `rally` board now contradict. This is exactly the test-invisible, player-facing drift the re-verify lane catches, on a system Brian specifically asked for ("events must be playable scenarios"). Reconciled **Guide_26 §3**: the milestone-headlines closing line now points forward to the one dark-side beat that *does* surface, and a new **"Dark-side cult uprisings — a beat you can fight"** subsection documents the full live loop — the menace meter + win/lose state (community breaks the cult before its deadline, or it wins), the `rally` / `rally strike` (alias `front`) surface, and the two live shapes the engine actually runs: **staged operations** (the Cult of the Hollow Sun / Tatooine, the Ember Court / Geonosis, the Ashen Hand / Coruscant — `rally` locates the live site, you go there and `investigate` stage by stage) vs **galaxy-wide menace cults** (the Drowned Choir / Nar Shaddaa, the Iron Veil / Kuat — `rally strike` rolls your best cross-playstyle pool, one counted strike per ~10 min, a community win never a solo macro). The reward is pinned to the engine: **Republic reputation** (≈3 for a single strike up to 15 for the largest contributor) + a commemorative status flag for the major contributors, and **no credits** (a reputation-and-roleplay beat; a loss costs nothing). Added a `rally` row to **§11** (the Director-related commands). Docs+test only; `parser/communal_commands.py` / `engine/communal_objective.py` / `engine/staged_event.py` / `engine/communal_objective_runtime.py` all READ-ONLY; era-clean; no schema/faucet/sink. New `tests/test_guide_26_cult_uprising_reverify.py` (24): pins the prose teaches the `rally`/`rally strike`/`front` surface + the menace meter + the staged-vs-menace split + the travel→`investigate` flow + the 5 named cults and their 5 worlds + the Republic-rep-only reward; and pins the **live backing** — `rally` and `investigate` resolve against the full registry, `STAGED_CULTS` == {hollow_sun, ember_court, ashen_hand}, `CULT_ROSTER` is the 5 named keys, `REP_FACTION/REP_FLOOR/REP_MAX` == republic/3/15, and `_distribute_rewards` never touches `adjust_credits` (the "no credits" promise) — so an engine revert or a guide drift fails loudly here. ### 2026-06-26 — Guides re-verify: Guide_16 §4 Step 1 teaches the visible tutorial soft-lock — *drop guide16-step1-softlock-reverify*
GUIDES re-verify lane (Opus-owned). Two path-to-first-fun fixes shipped *after* Guide_16's last pass, both squarely on the tutorial's **first action** — and the new-player guide (the doc a stuck newcomer actually reads) still described the old behavior. Republic Soldier step 1 gates `talk Major Tarrn` behind `requires_first: [look, +sheet]`. (1) The FUN2 tutorial soft-lock drop (`5ef1d17`, `engine/chain_events.py` + `parser/npc_commands.py` + `static/spa/m3_onboard.js`) fixed a stranding "kills-it": that gate used to fail *silently* — a newcomer who typed the onboarding panel's spotlighted `talk Major Tarrn` first got refused with no feedback. The fix made the gate VISIBLE three ways: `build_onboarding_state` now emits a `prereqs` checklist + `prereqs_met` so the panel ticks off `look`/`+sheet` and **locks the TYPE chip** until both are done, and `npe_prereq_hint` makes `TalkCommand` SPEAK the unmet prereq (*"Major Tarrn isn't ready for you yet — do `look`, `+sheet` first, then talk again."*) instead of stonewalling. (2) The multi-word-talk drop (`cfc3477`, `parser/npc_commands.py`) made `talk Major Tarrn` — the literal `command_to_type` the panel spotlights — resolve the full name to the NPC (it previously split on the first word and said "Tarrn" *to* "Major"). Guide_16 §4 Step 1 still wrote `talk Tarrn` and "when you've done both," teaching neither the visible gate nor the recovery hint. Reconciled §4 Step 1: it now teaches that the look→`+sheet`→talk order is enforced *and made visible* (the panel checklist + the locked TYPE chip), quotes Major Tarrn's spoken "isn't ready for you yet" hint verbatim so a player who talks too early reads it as feedback rather than a dead end, and teaches the literal `talk Major Tarrn` (the full multi-word name now resolves — you don't have to shorten it to `talk Tarrn`). Version 1.2 → 1.3. Docs+test only; `engine/chain_events.py`/`parser/npc_commands.py`/data READ-ONLY; era-clean; no schema/faucet/sink. New `TestStep1SoftLockTeaching` in `tests/test_guide_16_tutorial_chains.py` (7): the prose quotes the spoken hint + teaches the checklist + teaches the multi-word `talk Major Tarrn`; the chains.yaml step 1 still gates on `look`+`+sheet` with `command_to_type: "talk Major Tarrn"`; and the live backing holds — `npe_prereq_hint` still emits the "isn't ready for you yet" line, `npc_commands` still speaks it, and `build_onboarding_state` still emits the `prereqs`/`prereqs_met` checklist — so re-arming the silent gate or drifting the guide back to `talk Tarrn`-only fails loudly here. 63 guide-16 tests green.

### 2026-06-26 — Guides re-verify: Guide_03 §3 teaches the bare-`attack` auto-target — *drop guide03-bare-attack-reverify*
GUIDES re-verify lane (Opus-owned). The FUN2 combat first-fight drop (`cd90ddc`, `parser/combat_commands.py`) — a path-to-first-fun fix — made a bare `attack` (no target) auto-target the first hostile NPC in the room, so a newcomer following the tutorial panel's *literal* hint (which just says `attack`) lands a hit instead of getting a usage error. That shipped *after* Guide_03's last pass, and §3 — where a player learns the `attack` command — had drifted behind it: the command table read `attack <target>` ("Attack a specific target") and the targeting prose opened "Type the target's name after `attack`", both implying a target is mandatory. A new player reading the guide would never learn that the bare `attack` the tutorial tells them to type actually works — exactly the test-invisible, new-player-facing drift the re-verify lane catches. Reconciled §3: the command-table row is now `attack [target]` — "Attack a target — bare `attack` auto-targets the nearest hostile"; a new **"You don't have to name a target"** paragraph teaches that the bare `attack` is the tutorial panel's literal hint, that the game swings at the first hostile in the room (combat-sim droid / aggressive NPC / attacks-on-sight creature), and — crucially — that with nothing hostile present it shows the usage instead of guessing, so you only ever auto-target a real threat, never a bystander, friendly NPC, or another player; the following "Picking a target when names collide" subsection now opens "When you do name a target…" so the two flow. Docs+test only; `parser/combat_commands.py` READ-ONLY; era-clean; no schema/faucet/sink. New `tests/test_guide_03_bare_attack_reverify.py` (10): drives the live `AttackCommand._auto_target_hostile` and pins that it returns the first *hostile* NPC name, skips friendlies for a hostile, and returns `None` (→ usage help) when the room has no hostile; pins producer→consumer that `execute()` routes a bare `attack` through `_auto_target_hostile` and falls back to `_usage_help` on `None`; and pins the guide prose teaches the auto-target + the "never a bystander" safety + the optional-target command-table row — so an engine revert or a guide drift fails loudly here. 71 tests green across the new slice + the §3 disambiguation re-verify + the guide-03 authoritative suite + the FUN2 combat-firstfight suite.

### 2026-06-26 — T3.24 generalized questline: "The Dust-Sick" (fifth accessible side-questline) — *drop generalized-questline-dust-sick*
T3.24 generalized quest expansion (Opus-owned right-of-launch lane), fifth slice — author MORE questlines on the proven pattern per `quest_expansion_postlaunch_path_v1.md`. "The Dust-Sick" (`jundland_dust_sick`) is the FIFTH accessible (non-t5, non-tutorial) `kind: questline` chain, after The Ghost Shipment (Nar Shaddaa), The Crooked Wheel (Tatooine/Mos Eisley), The Lost Courier (Coruscant lower city), and The Skimmed Line (Kuat ring). It reuses the SAME live questline engine (`engine/tutorial_chains.py` + `chain_events.py`: the `kind: questline` type + `active_questline` slot, the existing event types, the four reward funnels) with **NO new engine code, no new slot, no new event type, no schema migration, no new reward faucet, and NO map-coordinate or exit-graph change** — pure additive content. **Two firsts for the accessible-questline arc:** (1) the **FIRST questline set in the WILDERNESS** — the Tatooine deep desert (the City Outskirts scavenger market and the Jundland Wastes), not a city interior like the four prior arcs; the desert rooms already exist + are loaded, so only NPCs + a chain + an achievement are added. (2) a **FIFTH distinct skill spread — FIRST_AID + SURVIVAL** (vs search+streetwise / investigation+gambling / persuasion+sneak / security+bargain), the first to lead with the medical/wilderness skills the combat and social arcs never exercise. It is also a new **story shape** — a save-the-stricken frontier rescue, not a racket-bust, smuggling-trace, kidnap-rescue, or industrial-theft: a homesteader's grown son went into the Wastes after a salvage lead and is down with "dust-sickness," and you stabilize him (`first_aid`), read the rising sand for a survivable line home (`survival`), drive off a scavenger band (`attack`/`dodge`), and walk him back to his mother. Like the prior four it is **ACCESSIBLE** — any post-chargen character can take it (prerequisites: `chargen_complete`; no faction rep gate), runs in normal un-secured desert rooms, faction-neutral (independent), and pays a modest reward (300 cr graduation + a one-time step-4 150 cr = 450 cr; 17 `independent` rep < the tuned 22 ceiling) + a lower-tier achievement (`dust_sick_recovered`, `cp_reward` 3, matching the prior four vs the t5 trainer chains' 5). 5 steps (talk → first_aid check → survival check → combat_won → talk) reusing existing event types; 2 original NPCs (Soree Bann, a Gran far-claim moisture-homesteader, the non-hostile GIVER + closer at the City Outskirts Scavenger Market, distinct from the market's ambient traders; Vohrek Sarn, a red-Nikto desert scavenger-boss, the step-4 combat foil carrying `chain_enemy_template: jundland_dust_scavenger_boss`). The stricken son **Eddin Bann is narrated-only** — a step-2/3/5 story beat, not a spawned NPC entity (the rescue target needs no producer). All step `location` slugs are real loaded Tatooine rooms (`outskirts_scavenger_market`/`jundland_hidden_cave`/`jundland_dune_sea_edge`/`jundland_beggars_canyon`), none reused from the Crooked Wheel; the NPC file is wired into `era.yaml content_refs`. Era-clean ~20 BBY, WEG R&E D6 stats — `first_aid` resolves to Technical and `survival` to Knowledge via the canonical `perform_skill_check` funnel (`canonical_skill_key`); both are registered in `data/skills.yaml`. Gran and red-Nikto are long-settled Tatooine-region species (era-clean, faction-neutral desert folk, no canon figures). Giver matched to the non-combat fixer band (Yelza Korrin/Dorae Vint/Sashi Renko/Dav Nuro), scavenger-boss to the small-time-criminal antagonist band (Brakkis Vone/Gorvahn Brusk/Skarn Vex/Suro Vant) — a real fight, beatable by a fresh character. Files: new `data/worlds/clone_wars/npcs_drop_generalized_questline_dust_sick.yaml` (+ era ref), additive chain in `data/worlds/clone_wars/tutorials/chains.yaml`, achievement in `data/achievements.yaml`. New `tests/test_generalized_questline_dust_sick.py` (17) walks start→graduation through the production dispatcher (the live parser's hooks), pins accessibility/reward-band/achievement/real-room-slugs/giver-presence/combat-template/era-wiring + the wrong-skill-no-advance guard + the first_aid→survival spread; the generic `test_t5_questline_content.TestAllQuestlinesWalkable` + the reachability invariant auto-cover it too (gate green: 17-test slice + 48 questline-walkability/siblings + 24 slice+reachability + hygiene + smoke).

### 2026-06-26 — T3.23 party skill challenges Phase 1: skill-gated anomaly phases wired live — *drop t3-23-party-skill-gate-phase1*
T3.23 party skill challenges (Opus-owned right-of-launch lane), **Phase 1 — the engine seam**, per `docs/design/party_skill_challenges_design_v1.md` §3/§4/§5/§7. The pre-launch drop (`t3-23-skill-gate-phase0`) landed the `skill_gate` anomaly-phase field INERT; this drop wires it to live skill-check resolution so a multi-phase wilderness anomaly can interleave **COMBAT phases** (advance on the last hostile's death — existing machinery) with **SKILL-GATE phases** (advance when a present character passes the gated check via `investigate <id>`). This is the substrate for D&D-style skill-diverse team content where a rogue/face/medic/demolitions-hand/fighter each have a phase to shine. **EXTENDS `engine/wilderness_anomalies.py` only — no new top-level system, command, schema migration, or reward faucet** (design §6 non-invasive scoping). Existing combat-only anomalies are byte-identical (no `skill_gate` key → unchanged path; 229-test anomaly regression green).
- **Skill-gate resolution (`_resolve_skill_gate_phase`):** `investigate <id>` on a challenge whose CURRENT phase has a `skill_gate` now attempts that gate — the team's specialist steps up. The character rolls whichever of the gate's `{skill}` ∪ `alt_skills` they're trained in (role substitution — the rogue picks the lock the demolitions hand would breach) via the canonical `perform_skill_check` funnel, against the gate `difficulty`. On success the phase clears, the contributor is logged, and the encounter advances (next phase) or, on the final phase, pays out. `resolve_anomaly` routes to it only when `phase_skill_gate(current_phase)` is set; combat phases of the same challenge fall through to the existing combat path (driven by `attack`/kills, never a re-investigate).
- **Soft-require / `solo_penalty` (design §4):** a lone wolf (no teammate present in the anchor room, via `_is_solo_engagement`) faces the gate at `difficulty + solo_penalty`; a party each clears their specialty at base difficulty. No hard `min_party_size` lock — soloable but punishing, so low population never produces dead content. Solo-detect fails toward NOT penalizing on any DB hiccup.
- **Retry-allowed with a time cost (design §8.2):** a missed gate is always retry-allowed, throttled by `SKILL_GATE_RETRY_COOLDOWN_SECS` (12s, module-tunable) — a frustration-control time cost, not a hard abort; the anomaly's expiry window remains the real fail condition. The cooldown clears on success.
- **Participation extension (design §5):** new `WildernessAnomaly.contribution_log` (per-char skill-gate clears) parallels `kill_counts`. `_payout_combat_anomaly` now enumerates participants as the **UNION** of combat killers and skill-gate clearers (both T2 room-occupant and T3 kill-count paths), so a slicer/medic/face who only cleared a non-combat phase earns the same participation-scaled reward share as the fighters. No-op for existing combat-only anomalies (empty `contribution_log`).
- Phase-advance unified: `_advance_to_next_phase` now begins a skill-gate next phase (moves the pointer + broadcasts the intro + returns True, awaiting an investigate) instead of the old inert `return False`; combat next phases unchanged. Intro/clear room broadcasts refactored into `_broadcast_phase_intro` / `_broadcast_skill_gate_clear` (best-effort, no-op without a session_mgr). `investigate` command renders the new `skill_gate` mode (roll vs the possibly-solo-penalized DC + any final-clear reward); `resolve_anomaly` + the command thread `session_mgr`.
- **Scope:** this is the engine seam only — the first AUTHORED production party challenge (design Phase 2) + its spawn-cadence/reward-balance integration is a deliberate follow-up drop (a mis-tuned auto-spawning end-game challenge shouldn't land unattended). So this drop adds **zero** live `TIER3_TEMPLATES` (the 4-template catalog count is unchanged); the challenge templates exist only in-test, exercising the full seam with zero live-cadence perturbation.
- Era-clean ~20 BBY, WEG R&E D6 (all checks via `perform_skill_check`); `force_sensitive` untouched. The stale `test_t3_23_skill_gate_phase0.py` inert-seam guards (`TestInertness` + the advance-returns-False assertion) were updated to the now-active behavior. New `tests/test_t3_23_party_skill_gate_phase1.py` (33): routing + attribution, clear-advances + contribution credit, fail/retry/cooldown-throttle, solo-penalty (taxed solo / base with a teammate / no-penalty gate), alt-skill substitution + primary preference + untrained fallback, final-gate payout + skill-only-participant, a full skill→combat→skill end-to-end walk unioning fighter + slicer participants, and combat-only behavior-neutrality.

### 2026-06-25 — Tutorial sim safe-sandbox: the drill can't kill, strand, or scar the learner — *drop fun3-sim-safety*
Fixes the last first-fun blocker from the 3rd fun re-run (score 4/10): the "non-lethal" Combat Sim drill ONE-SHOT the new player (B1 Sim Droid Damage 19 vs Soak 8 → Incapacitated round 1), then hard-dead-ended (no recovery, exits gone, impossible objective) and applied permanent facial scars — in a drill the NPC calls non-lethal. Brian's call: make the sim a genuine SAFE SANDBOX via a reusable `is_simulation` room flag. **Implementation:** `tipoca_combat_sim` gains `properties.is_simulation: true` (additive world-YAML); `CombatInstance` carries `is_simulation`, set at creation from `get_room_property(room_id,'is_simulation')` via `_setup_combat` (fail-safe → normal combat on any lookup error); a new branch in `_apply_damage` caps a **PLAYER** defender (`not target_c.is_npc`) to STUNNED/KO (margin>3 → the auto-clearing unconscious-KO; margin 1-3 → STUNNED) — **NPCs take normal damage so the drill stays winnable**; `add_scar` (parser/combat_commands.py) is skipped when the combat is_simulation. **code-review caught a BLOCKER, fixed:** `apply_wound(STUNNED)` ITSELF escalates `wound_level` to INCAPACITATED once `stun_timers >= STR dice` (R&E p83 stun-KO), so repeated sim hits re-broke the guarantee after ~3 rounds — the sim branch now clamps `wound_level` back to STUNNED after the stun (the auto-clearing `unconscious_until` is the only "down" in a sim, never a persisted wound; stun_timers still accrue for the awake −1D). Verified: 19 tests (incl. the multi-hit-accumulation regression that proves the blocker fix) + a live Playwright drive of the actual tutorial drill — the player reaches the flagged Combat Simulator, mows the droids down ("HIT — Dead!"), is never incapacitated/scarred, and can act throughout (was one-shot before). 293 existing combat tests green; non-sim combat byte-identical. NOTE: the Geonosis "live-fire drill pit" was deliberately NOT flagged (the name implies real stakes); the empty `#combat-feed` HUD is a separate tier-3 finding. WEG D6, era-clean, no faucet/sink. New `tests/test_fun3_sim_safety.py` (19).

### 2026-06-25 — Rank-up toast: ship the phantom `benefits` sub-field its consumer already renders — *drop rank-up-benefits* (Opus loop)
Closes another producer/consumer gap from the launch-readiness "phantom/dead consumer" backlog, resolved the **best/most-complete** way (add the producer, not delete the consumer). `static/client.html::handleRankUp(data)` renders the promotion toast as `showToast({head:'⬆ RANK UP · '+faction, body:data.new_rank, sub:data.benefits || ''})` — but the **only** producer of the `rank_up` event (`engine/organizations.check_auto_promote`) emitted `{faction, new_rank, new_level}` with **no `benefits` key**, so the toast's sub-line was *always blank*: every promotion the player ever saw on the web client lost the "what did I just earn" line. Fix: a new `engine/organizations.rank_benefits_summary(faction_code, rank, rank_level)` derives a concise, accurate, player-facing summary from the rank's **real** grants — its weekly stipend (`STIPEND_TABLE`) plus the equipment it newly issues (resolved to `EQUIPMENT_CATALOG` display names, unknown codes falling back to the raw code) — and crucially mirrors `promote()`'s own issuance gate (spec-eligible factions defer rank-1 gear to `SpecializeCommand`, so the summary doesn't promise gear that won't be issued). `check_auto_promote` now computes the summary once and ships it in BOTH surfaces: the web `rank_up` event gains a `benefits` field (key always present — empty string when a rank confers neither stipend nor gear, so the SPA consumer never reads `undefined`), and the telnet line gains a matching `Benefits: …` row for parity (only when non-empty). Producer-only change at the existing emit site; `promote()` and the achievement/housing hooks are untouched; no schema, no faucet/sink, era-clean (stipend + gear names only). New `tests/test_rank_up_benefits_producer.py` (13: summary covers stipend-only / stipend+equipment (catalog name not raw code) / unknown-code fallback / many-items "+ N more" / spec-faction rank-1 deferral / malformed-JSON safety / no-benefits-empty, and the producer carries a non-empty `benefits` in the event, echoes it on the telnet line, and always includes the key even when empty — driving the REAL `check_auto_promote` via a fake DB+session). Gate green (13 new + achievement-hook + hygiene + smoke foundation = 27 passed).

### 2026-06-25 — Login UX: emit the structured `auth_status` event the SPA already consumes — *drop auth-status-producer* (Opus loop)
Closes a real producer/consumer gap from the launch-readiness "dead consumer" backlog — but resolved the **best/most-complete** way (add the producer), not by deleting the consumer. `static/client.html` has a complete CONSUMER for a structured auth result — `handleAuthStatus(msg)` (`case 'auth_status'`) reading `{ok, kind, username?, reason?, message?}`, documented as *"Structured auth result from server (replaces regex matching on text)"* — but an exhaustive grep proved **no server path ever emitted `auth_status`** (the only two references in the whole tree were the client comment + the client `case`). So the SPA login form ran ENTIRELY on the brittle `checkForAuthFailure` regex-on-English-text fallback, which only pattern-matches a few error wordings and — critically — does **not** match the per-IP rate-limit notice, so a rate-limited web login hung the form in its `awaitingAuth` state forever with no error shown (a real latent UX bug). Fix: a new fail-open, telnet-safe `GameServer._emit_auth_status` helper, called on EVERY connect/create outcome in `handle_new_session` — connect success / bad-credentials / rate-limited, create success / username-taken / username-too-short / password-too-short / rate-limited, plus the `__token_auth__` web-handoff success (9 sites). Failures emit the structured event BEFORE the text line so the SPA resets `awaitingAuth` deterministically regardless of wording; the legacy text + regex fallback stays in place for old servers but is no longer depended on. `Session.send_json` silently drops unknown types for non-WebSocket transports, so the change is purely additive for telnet (no JSON leaks). No schema, no faucet/sink, era-clean; the payload mirrors the consumer's documented contract (`ok`/`kind`/`username`/`reason`/`message`). New `tests/test_auth_status_producer.py` (9: drives the REAL login loop via the proven WS-session harness — connect-success ok / bad-creds failure / rate-limited structured failure (the latent-bug regression guard) / create-success ok / username-taken / short-username validation failure / telnet emits NO JSON but still gets the text error / producer wired at ≥8 branches / SPA consumer contract still present). Gate green (9 new + preauth-throttle + token-bypass + hygiene + smoke foundation = 43 passed).
